--- Commit Message ---
LU-18456 mdt: create trash dir for MDT after MDT stack setup

In this patch, the trash directories corresponding to various MDTs
are created in mdt_postrecov(). The trash directory should be
visible in Lustre namespace by the path ".lustre/.ltrash/MDTXXXX".

The ".lustre/.ltrash/MDT0000" is a local trash directory for MDT0.
However, for the trash directories ".lustre/.ltrash/MDTXXXX"
corresponding to MDTs other than MDT0, they are all remote
directories with parent ".lustre/.ltrash" (FID: LU_TRASH_FID) on
MDT0.

Moreover, the trash directory is created in an extra thread in
->o_postrecov() to avoid blocking the MDT stack setup and
recovery. The reasons are as follows:
- all devices belonging to the MDT stack are configured and setup
  properly.
- The recovery procedure is finished, and the server becomes
  ready to handle the normal RPCs (i.e. create remote directory
  for a trash directory).

Address the review comments on this patch:
- Give the trash setup kthread a session and server credentials;
  without them lu_ucred() is NULL in mdd_create_object().
- Back off unconditionally before retrying and check lgt_abort
  before the retry, so a persistent failure neither spins the
  thread nor delays an unmount; report the error only when it
  changes.
- Clear lgt_init in lu_generic_thread_stop() so that the helper
  can be started again after it was waited for.

Change-Id: Ibf2598f6b0e1b543755d68ffeb669c19be4bf018
Signed-off-by: Qian Yingjin <qian@ddn.com>
Signed-off-by: Xiyang Wang <xiwang@ddn.com>

Patch Set 18:

(5 comments)

AI code review results below, please doublecheck

Needs a refresh: as written, the trash setup thread never succeeds in creating .lustre/.ltrash/MDTxxxx, a umount during recovery can leave the thread running against a freed mdt_device, and an existing MDT0 can no longer be mounted read-only. See inline comments.

Nothing in this patch checks that .lustre/.ltrash/MDTxxxx actually gets created. A small sanity/conf-sanity case that looks it up on each MDT after mount would have caught the -EACCES problem. Also, the PS14 request to fold the follow-on fixes into this patch still seems open: 61978 says this patch on its own makes sanity-scrub test_4b fail.
